# Every topic: detailed coverage checklist


This checklist retains original wording of granular entries and weekly lesson rows. Only exact normalized duplicates are grouped; full originals remain in the notes. A row may contain several subtopics: check it only when every named part is covered. For finer tracking, record a subtopic in your Notes field. Reading and mastery are separate.

- [ ] What an array is — [detail 1](NOTES/01_DSA_FULL_NOTES.md#QA-B0003), [detail 2](NOTES/01_DSA_FULL_NOTES.md#QA-B0158)

- [ ] An array is a fixed-size, strongly typed collection that stores multiple elements of the same type. Each element is accessed using an index, and array indexing provides O(1) access by index. In C#, arrays are reference types, even when they contain value types. — [detail 1](NOTES/01_DSA_FULL_NOTES.md#QA-B0004)

## Complete AI, MCP and RAG learning notes


- [ ] Where AI genuinely helps — Boilerplate, test scaffolding, unfamiliar API syntax, first-draft documentation, explaining unfamiliar code, refactoring with clear intent, regex, SQL translation — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0014)

- [ ] Where AI reliably hurts — Novel algorithms, security-sensitive code, anything requiring current library versions, subtle concurrency, business rules it cannot see, and any case where you cannot evaluate the output — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0014)

- [ ] The verification discipline — You own every line you commit. Read it, understand it, test it. "Copilot wrote it" is not a defence in code review or in production — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0014)

- [ ] Context engineering — Why these tools fail on large codebases, how to give them the right context, why a focused prompt with the relevant file beats a vague prompt with none — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0014)

- [ ] Review-mode usage — The highest-value pattern for a senior engineer: write it yourself, then ask the model to critique it. Inverts the risk — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0014)

- [ ] Failure modes — Hallucinated APIs, plausible-but-wrong concurrency, outdated patterns, confident nonsense on niche topics, silent security holes — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0014)

- [ ] Tokens — Text is split into tokens, not words. Pricing, limits and truncation are all token-based — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0019)

- [ ] Context window — The working memory of a request. Everything - system prompt, history, retrieved documents - competes for it — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0019)

- [ ] Embeddings — Text mapped to a vector such that semantic similarity becomes geometric distance. This is the foundation of RAG — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0019)

- [ ] Vector similarity — Cosine similarity, dot product, Euclidean. Why cosine is usually chosen — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0019)

- [ ] Temperature and sampling — Determinism vs variety. Why you use low temperature for extraction and classification — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0019)

- [ ] System vs user messages — Role separation and why instructions in the system message behave differently — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0019)

- [ ] Structured output — Forcing JSON conforming to a schema. The single most useful feature for backend integration — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0019)

- [ ] Function/tool calling — The model requests that your code run a function and returns the result into its reasoning. The mechanism underneath agents — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0019)

- [ ] Streaming — Token-by-token responses, and what that means for your API design and timeouts — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0019)

- [ ] Cost and latency — Per-token pricing, prompt caching, why prompt size is a performance problem — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0019)

- [ ] Hallucination — Why models produce confident wrong answers, and why grounding via retrieval is the mitigation — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0019)

- [ ] Ingest — Load documents - PDF, DOCX, HTML, code. Extraction quality sets a ceiling on everything downstream. Bad PDF parsing is the most common silent RAG failure — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0024)

- [ ] Chunk — Split into passages. Fixed size, recursive, semantic, or structure-aware. Chunk size and overlap are the highest-leverage tuning knobs. Too small loses context, too large dilutes the embedding — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0024)

- [ ] Embed — Each chunk to a vector via an embedding model. Same model must be used at query time — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0024)

- [ ] Store — Vectors plus metadata in a vector store. Index type (HNSW, IVF) trades recall against speed and memory — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0024)

- [ ] Retrieve — Embed the query, find top-K nearest chunks. K is a tuning decision — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0024)

- [ ] Rerank — Optionally re-score candidates with a stronger model. Usually the biggest single quality improvement after chunking — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0024)

- [ ] Augment — Build the prompt: instructions, retrieved context, question. Manage the context budget — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0024)

- [ ] Generate — Model answers from the provided context, and is instructed to say when the context is insufficient — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0024)

- [ ] Cite — Return sources. Non-negotiable for trust, and it makes the system debuggable — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0024)

- [ ] Host — The application the user interacts with - an IDE, a desktop assistant, an agent runtime. Contains one or more clients — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0039)

- [ ] Client — Maintains a one-to-one connection with a server, handles the protocol — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0039)

- [ ] Server — Exposes capabilities. Usually a small process you write, wrapping an API, database or filesystem — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0039)

- [ ] Tools — Functions the model can invoke - model-controlled. This is the primary primitive. "Create a ticket", "run a query" — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0039)

- [ ] Resources — Data the host can read - application-controlled. Files, records, documents — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0039)

- [ ] Prompts — Reusable templates the user can invoke - user-controlled. Slash commands, essentially — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0039)

- [ ] Transport — stdio for local processes; HTTP-based transport for remote servers. Messages are JSON-RPC — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0039)

- [ ] Capability negotiation — Client and server declare what they support during initialisation — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0039)

- [ ] 1 (Wk 25) — ASP.NET Core API. Document ingest endpoint. Text extraction. Chunking with configurable size and overlap. Store chunks in Postgres. Reuse your job platform's worker pattern for async ingestion — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0052)

- [ ] 2 (Wk 26) — Embeddings generation. pgvector column and index. Vector similarity search. POST /query returning top-K chunks with scores. No model call yet - prove retrieval works before adding generation — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0052)

- [ ] 3 (Wk 27) — Generation with grounded prompting and citations. Structured JSON output. Streaming response. "I do not know" behaviour when context is insufficient — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0052)

- [ ] 4 (Wk 28) — Hybrid search (BM25 + vector, fused). A small evaluation set of ~20 question/answer pairs with recall@k measured. Metadata filtering for multi-tenancy. Caching. README with architecture, trade-offs and evaluation results — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0052)

- [ ] Optional — An MCP server exposing your job platform as tools - submit a job, check status, inspect the DLQ. Small, and it demonstrates both protocols working together — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0052)

- [ ] Microsoft Learn - AI for .NET developers learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/ai — The canonical .NET path. Microsoft.Extensions.AI, vector data abstractions, tutorials — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0056)

- [ ] Semantic Kernel docs and GitHub — Microsoft's orchestration framework for .NET. Agents, plugins, memory — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0056)

- [ ] Kernel Memory (GitHub) — Microsoft's open-source RAG service. Read the source - it is a well-built reference implementation — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0056)

- [ ] Model Context Protocol modelcontextprotocol.io — The official specification. Read the spec, not summaries of it — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0056)

- [ ] MCP C# SDK github.com/modelcontextprotocol/csharp-sdk — Build MCP servers in .NET — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0056)

- [ ] Anthropic documentation docs.anthropic.com — Prompting, tool use, structured output, context management. Applies broadly — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0056)

- [ ] pgvector github.com/pgvector/pgvector — Vector search in the database you already know — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0056)

- [ ] Anthropic and OpenAI cookbooks (GitHub) — Working patterns for RAG, evaluation and tool use — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0056)

- [ ] Free tiers — Most providers have free or low-cost tiers sufficient for a study project. Local embedding models via ONNX avoid API cost entirely — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0056)

- [ ] M2 Sat (one block) — Track A. Draft your "how do you use AI tools" answer — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0058)

- [ ] M3 Sat (one block) — Track B. LLM fundamentals. Tokens, embeddings, tool calling, structured output — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0058)

- [ ] M4 Sat (one block) — Track C theory. The RAG pipeline and the failure taxonomy — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0058)

- [ ] M5 — Nothing. Month 5 belongs to the main project and behavioural. Protect it — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0058)

- [ ] M7 Sat x4 — The RAG build, phases 1-4 — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0058)

- [ ] M8 — Rehearse explaining it. Optional MCP server if time genuinely allows — [detail 1](NOTES/10_AI_MCP_RAG_FULL_NOTES.md#S12-B0058)

## Complete quantitative, logical, verbal and estimation notes


- [ ] Where it matters — Reality — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0005)

- [ ] Google SWE loop — Classical aptitude is not tested. No quant round, no logical reasoning round — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0005)

- [ ] Amazon SDE loop — Classical aptitude is not tested in the technical loop. Amazon's assessments do include work-style and work-simulation components, which are not aptitude tests — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0005)

- [ ] Mid-tier and Indian product companies — Frequently tested. Online assessments commonly include quantitative aptitude, logical reasoning and verbal sections as a gate before the technical rounds — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0005)

- [ ] Service companies and mass recruiters — Heavily tested, often as the primary filter — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0005)

- [ ] System design (both targets) — Quantitative estimation matters a great deal. Back-of-envelope capacity estimation is step 2 of the nine-step framework — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0005)

- [ ] Number system — Divisibility rules, factors, LCM and HCF, remainders, unit digits, base conversion — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0009)

- [ ] Percentages — Increase/decrease, successive percentages, percentage change vs point change — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0009)

- [ ] Ratio and proportion — Direct and inverse, partnership, mixtures and alligation — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0009)

- [ ] Averages — Weighted averages, effect of adding or removing a term — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0009)

- [ ] Profit and loss — Cost/selling price, discount chains, marked price, false weights — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0009)

- [ ] Simple and compound interest — Formulae, half-yearly and quarterly compounding, the difference between SI and CI — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0009)

- [ ] Time, speed and distance — Relative speed, trains, boats and streams, average speed — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0009)

- [ ] Time and work — Work rates, combined work, efficiency, pipes and cisterns — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0009)

- [ ] Permutations and combinations — Arrangements, selections, circular arrangements, repetitions — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0009)

- [ ] Probability — Basic, conditional, complementary events, dice and cards — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0009)

- [ ] Mensuration — Areas, volumes, surface areas of standard shapes — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0009)

- [ ] Progressions — AP and GP, sums, nth term — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0009)

- [ ] Data interpretation — Tables, bar charts, pie charts, line graphs, caselets. Highest frequency section in most assessments — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0009)

- [ ] Number and letter series — Pattern identification, missing terms — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0013)

- [ ] Analogies and classification — Relationship identification, odd one out — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0013)

- [ ] Coding-decoding — Letter shifts, substitution, symbol coding — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0013)

- [ ] Blood relations — Family tree diagramming — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0013)

- [ ] Direction sense — Displacement and final position — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0013)

- [ ] Syllogisms — Venn diagram method. Learn the diagram method, do not reason verbally - verbal reasoning on syllogisms is where people lose marks — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0013)

- [ ] Seating arrangement — Linear, circular, facing inward/outward. Grid method — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0013)

- [ ] Puzzles — Floor puzzles, scheduling, matrix puzzles. Highest time cost per question - triage these — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0013)

- [ ] Clocks and calendars — Angles, odd days, day-of-week calculation — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0013)

- [ ] Cubes and dice — Painted cubes, opposite faces — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0013)

- [ ] Statement and assumption/conclusion — Formal logic on prose — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0013)

- [ ] Data sufficiency — Judging whether the information suffices, without solving. Genuinely useful for interview requirement-clarification too — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0013)

- [ ] Power — Approximate value — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0031)

- [ ] 2^10 — 1 Thousand — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0031)

- [ ] 2^20 — 1 Million — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0031)

- [ ] 2^30 — 1 Billion — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0031)

- [ ] 2^40 — 1 Trillion — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0031)

- [ ] 2^50 — 1 Quadrillion — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0031)

- [ ] Operation — Rough order of magnitude — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0033)

- [ ] L1 cache reference — ~1 ns — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0033)

- [ ] Main memory reference — ~100 ns — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0033)

- [ ] SSD random read — ~100 microseconds — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0033)

- [ ] Round trip within the same datacentre — ~500 microseconds — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0033)

- [ ] Disk seek (spinning) — ~10 ms — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0033)

- [ ] Round trip India to US — ~150 ms — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0033)

- [ ] Given — Compute in under 2 minutes — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0036)

- [ ] 100M daily active users, 10 requests each — Average QPS, then peak QPS at 3x — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0036)

- [ ] 500M writes/day, 1 KB each — Daily storage, yearly storage, with replication factor 3 — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0036)

- [ ] 1M QPS reads, 10 KB responses — Bandwidth required — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0036)

- [ ] 10 TB of data, 1 TB per node — Node count, plus replication overhead — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0036)

- [ ] 100k concurrent WebSocket connections — Memory and connection budget per server, servers needed — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0036)

- [ ] IndiaBix indiabix.com — The standard free bank for quantitative, reasoning and verbal, with explanations — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0039)

- [ ] GeeksforGeeks - Aptitude — Topic-wise theory plus practice sets, plus their technical aptitude sections — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0039)

- [ ] PrepInsta (free sections) — Company-specific assessment patterns for Indian recruiters — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0039)

- [ ] Khan Academy — If any underlying maths is genuinely shaky - ratios, probability, algebra. Free and well built — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0039)

- [ ] Latency numbers every programmer should know — Widely available reference. Print it and keep it visible — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0039)

- [ ] System Design Primer - estimation section — Back-of-envelope worked examples — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0039)

- [ ] Month — Aptitude work — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0041)

- [ ] M1-M2 — Tue/Thu last 15 min. Quantitative fundamentals: number system, percentages, ratios, averages, profit/loss, TSD, time and work — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0041)

- [ ] M3-M4 — Tue/Thu 15 min. Logical reasoning: series, syllogisms (diagram method), seating, puzzles, blood relations, directions. Plus estimation drills inside every system design — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0041)

- [ ] M5 — Tue/Thu 15 min. Data interpretation, which is the highest-frequency section. Plus verbal light touch — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0041)

- [ ] M6 Weeks 21-22 — Intensive: 30 min/day for two weeks. Full timed mock assessments before the mid-tier applications land. This is when it actually pays — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0041)

- [ ] M7-M8 — Maintenance only. One timed set per week if assessments are still coming in — [detail 1](NOTES/11_APTITUDE_FULL_NOTES.md#S13-B0041)

- [ ] Situation — Correct response — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0070)

- [ ] Missed 2-3 days — Ignore it. Carry on from today. Do not catch up — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0070)

- [ ] Missed a week — Skip forward to the current week. Put the missed topics in the WEAK tab. The review ladder will catch them — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0070)

- [ ] Missed two weeks — Same as above, plus move the start-date cell in the tracker forward two weeks. The plan is 6-8 months, not exactly 8. It absorbs this — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0070)

- [ ] Missed a month — Restart the current month. Do not restart the plan — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0070)

- [ ] Feeling behind constantly — You are doing too much per day. Cut using Rule 3. A smaller plan you complete beats a bigger plan you abandon — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0070)

- [ ] Number — Why this one — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0083)

- [ ] Days studied this month, out of available days — Consistency is the only real predictor — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0083)

- [ ] WEAK tab items cleared — Proves you are repairing, not just consuming — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0083)

- [ ] Concepts that passed the Day 30 review — Proves it is actually sticking — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0083)

- [ ] What is it? — One sentence a junior developer would understand — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0098)

- [ ] Why does it exist? — What problem forced its invention. Concepts without problems are unmemorable — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0098)

- [ ] How does it work internally? — The mechanism. This is the level Google and Amazon probe — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0098)

- [ ] What are the trade-offs? — Nothing is free. Name the cost — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0098)

- [ ] When would I use it? — A real scenario, ideally from your own work — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0098)

- [ ] When would I NOT use it? — The question that separates deep from shallow knowledge — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0098)

- [ ] Not progress — Progress — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0108)

- [ ] Watched a 3-hour .NET video — Explained the CLR loading process from memory, correctly — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0108)

- [ ] Read the LeetCode solution — Re-solved it from scratch 3 days later without hints — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0108)

- [ ] Read a system design article — Drew the design on paper in 45 min and named 3 bottlenecks — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0108)

- [ ] Reread notes on async/await — Wrote a deadlock, explained why, then fixed it — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0108)

- [ ] Resume screen — Recruiter or automated filter — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0116)

- [ ] Online assessment — Timed coding, typically 2 problems — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0116)

- [ ] Technical phone screen — 45 min, shared doc, one interviewer — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0116)

- [ ] Onsite loop — 4-6 rounds back to back — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0116)

- [ ] Hiring committee — Packet reviewed by people who never met you — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0116)

- [ ] Online assessment — DSA, plus for SDE II+ a 20-min system design sketch — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0120)

- [ ] Phone screen — Coding plus 1-2 Leadership Principle questions — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0120)

- [ ] Loop — DSA, LLD, HLD, with LP woven into every round — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0120)

- [ ] Bar Raiser — A trained interviewer from outside the hiring team — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0120)

- [ ] Area — Your state — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0123)

- [ ] Backend building — Strong — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0123)

- [ ] .NET depth — Shallow-to-mid — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0123)

- [ ] DSA — Near zero — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0123)

- [ ] System design — Near zero — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0123)

- [ ] LLD / OOD — Weak — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0123)

- [ ] CS fundamentals — Weak — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0123)

- [ ] Behavioural — Unstructured — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0123)

- [ ] Communication — Beginner+ — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0123)

- [ ] Portfolio project — None — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0123)

- [ ] Retention — Poor by your own account — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0123)

- [ ] Field — Content — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0129)

- [ ] Brute force — The obvious solution and its complexity — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0129)

- [ ] Optimised idea — The key insight in one sentence. If you cannot write it in one sentence, you have not understood the problem - you have only made it pass — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0129)

- [ ] Why it works — The invariant or property being exploited — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0129)

- [ ] Time / space — Both, with justification — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0129)

- [ ] Pattern name — Which of the 15 patterns below this is — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0129)

- [ ] One variation — How would this change if the input were sorted, had duplicates, or were a stream — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0129)

- [ ] Pattern — Recognise it when — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0132)

- [ ] Two pointers — Sorted array, pair/triplet sums, in-place partition — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0132)

- [ ] Sliding window — Contiguous subarray or substring with a constraint — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0132)

- [ ] Fast and slow pointers — Cycle detection, middle of list, happy number — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0132)

- [ ] Prefix sum / hashing — Subarray sums, counting, "seen before" lookups — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0132)

- [ ] Binary search on answer — Monotonic predicate, "minimum X such that..." — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0132)

- [ ] Monotonic stack — Next greater or smaller element, histogram, daily temperatures — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0132)

- [ ] Heap / top-K — K largest, merge K lists, running median — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0132)

- [ ] Intervals — Merge, insert, meeting rooms, sweep line — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0132)

- [ ] Tree DFS — Path problems, subtree aggregation, validate BST — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0132)

- [ ] Tree BFS — Level order, min depth, right side view — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0132)

- [ ] Graph BFS/DFS — Connectivity, shortest unweighted path, islands — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0132)

- [ ] Topological sort — Dependencies, course schedule, build order — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0132)

- [ ] Union-Find — Dynamic connectivity, number of components, redundant edge — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0132)

- [ ] Backtracking — Permutations, combinations, subsets, N-Queens, word search — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0132)

- [ ] Dynamic programming — Overlapping subproblems. 1D, 2D, knapsack, LIS, string DP — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0132)

- [ ] # — Step — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0137)

- [ ] 1 — Clarify requirements — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0137)

- [ ] 2 — Estimate scale — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0137)

- [ ] 3 — Define the API — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0137)

- [ ] 4 — Data model — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0137)

- [ ] 5 — High-level architecture — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0137)

- [ ] 6 — Identify bottlenecks — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0137)

- [ ] 7 — Scale it — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0137)

- [ ] 8 — Failure handling — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0137)

- [ ] 9 — Trade-offs — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0137)

- [ ] Month — You must be able to... — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0145)

- [ ] M1 — Explain value vs reference types, stack vs heap, boxing, and deferred execution - no notes. Solve easy array, string and hashing problems unaided — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0145)

- [ ] M2 — Explain the async/await state machine, the thread pool, and the ASP.NET Core request pipeline. Read a SQL execution plan. Solve easy-medium trees and linked lists — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0145)

- [ ] M3 — Explain end to end what happens when a browser requests a URL. Explain isolation levels and which anomalies each permits. Complete one system design in 45 minutes — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0145)

- [ ] M4 — Apply SOLID in a real design. Explain idempotency, DLQ and at-least-once delivery. Project phase 1 running end to end — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0145)

- [ ] M5 — THE GATE. Project deployed and load tested. 20 STAR stories written and spoken. 9 system designs done cold. Do not apply before this passes — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0145)

- [ ] M6 — Applying. Timed medium DSA consistently. A 45-minute design conversation without freezing. Project explained in 5 minutes — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0145)

- [ ] M7 — Converting interviews. Every rejection analysed into a specific named gap, and repaired — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0145)

- [ ] M8 — Consolidating. 10-15 designs from a blank page. All missed DSA problems re-solved from memory — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0145)

- [ ] Risk — Countermeasure — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0147)

- [ ] Reverting to rereading when tired — The session must open with retrieval. Non-negotiable, even at 10 minutes — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0147)

- [ ] DSA anxiety causing avoidance — DSA is every day, never skipped, never batched into weekends — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0147)

- [ ] Skipping system design because DSA feels more urgent — It is 50% of your level's bar. Thursday is design day and it is protected — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0147)

- [ ] Building the project as CRUD because it is comfortable — The distributed behaviour is the project. If it cannot survive a worker being killed mid-job, handle the same message twice, and show load-test numbers, it will not do its job in an interview — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0147)

- [ ] Tutorial consumption feeling like progress — The metric is what you produced from memory, never what you watched — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0147)

- [ ] Behavioural left until the end — The journal line starts today, not in week 17 — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0147)

- [ ] Burnout around weeks 9-16 — The plan has deliberate lighter weeks. Take them. Eight sustainable months beats three heroic ones — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0147)

- [ ] Applying too early and burning good companies — Week 21 is the earliest, and only after the week 20 gate. Use smaller companies first as calibration — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0147)

- [ ] Rebuilding the tracker instead of using it — It is already built. Reorganising your system is procrastination wearing a productive hat — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#ST-B0147)

- [ ] Deep block: WebSockets and SignalR. The upgrade handshake, framing, ping/pong, scaling stateful connections, backplane (Redis), sticky sessions, fallbacks. Connect to your VCDoctor SignalR work — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0014)

- [ ] Weekly test. Plan Week 10 — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0014)

- [ ] Concepts: representations (adjacency list vs matrix), BFS vs DFS, visited sets, connected components, grid-as-graph. — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0016)

- [ ] Problems: Number of Islands, Clone Graph, Max Area of Island, Pacific Atlantic Water Flow, Surrounded Regions, Rotting Oranges, Walls and Gates, Word Ladder, Number of Connected Components in an Undirected Graph, Graph Valid Tree. — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0017)

- [ ] Process vs thread at the OS level. Address space, PCB, context switching and its real cost. User mode vs kernel mode, system calls. Process lifecycle — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0019)

- [ ] Memory: virtual memory, paging, page tables, TLB, page faults, swapping, memory-mapped files. Stack vs heap at the OS level - and how that relates to what you learned in Month 1 — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0019)

- [ ] Scheduling: preemptive vs cooperative, time slices, priorities, starvation, round robin, MLFQ. Then map it: how does the .NET thread pool scheduler relate to the OS scheduler — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0019)

- [ ] Synchronisation at OS level: mutex vs semaphore vs spinlock, futex, atomic instructions (CAS), the deadlock conditions again from the OS side, priority inversion. IPC: pipes, shared memory, sockets — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0019)

- [ ] Deep block: file systems and I/O. Blocking vs non-blocking I/O, sync vs async I/O, select/epoll/IOCP, and how this underpins async in .NET. This closes the loop with Month 1 Week 4 — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0019)

- [ ] Weekly test. Plan Week 11 — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0019)

- [ ] Concepts: topological sort (Kahn's and DFS), cycle detection in directed graphs, union-find with path compression, Dijkstra. — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0021)

- [ ] Problems: Course Schedule, Course Schedule II, Alien Dictionary, Redundant Connection, Accounts Merge, Number of Provinces, Network Delay Time, Cheapest Flights Within K Stops, Min Cost to Connect All Points, Reconstruct Itinerary. — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0022)

- [ ] Replication: leader-follower, replication lag, read-your-writes, multi-leader, leaderless, quorum (R + W > N). Failover and split brain — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0024)

- [ ] Partitioning and sharding: by key range, by hash, consistent hashing and virtual nodes, hot partitions, rebalancing, secondary indexes in a sharded world — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0024)

- [ ] CAP and consistency: what CAP actually says (and the common misreading), PACELC, strong vs eventual vs causal consistency, linearizability. Conflict resolution: last-write-wins, vector clocks, CRDTs at a concept level — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0024)

- [ ] Caching in depth: cache-aside, read-through, write-through, write-behind, refresh-ahead. Eviction (LRU, LFU, TTL). Cache stampede, thundering herd, negative caching. Redis internals: single-threaded model, data structures, persistence (RDB/AOF), cluster mode — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0024)

- [ ] Deep block: SQL vs NoSQL honestly. Document, key-value, wide-column, graph stores. When each wins. Then: read unfamiliar code. Pick an open-source .NET repo, read a subsystem cold for 90 minutes, and write what it does — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0024)

- [ ] Weekly test. Plan Week 12 — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0024)

- [ ] Concepts: sorting as a preprocessing step, sweep line, exchange argument for greedy correctness. — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0026)

- [ ] Problems: Merge Intervals, Insert Interval, Non-overlapping Intervals, Meeting Rooms, Meeting Rooms II, Minimum Number of Arrows to Burst Balloons, Jump Game, Jump Game II, Gas Station, Partition Labels. — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0027)

- [ ] Queues and messaging. Point-to-point vs pub/sub. Producer/consumer. Acknowledgements, visibility timeout, at-most-once vs at-least-once vs "exactly-once". Why exactly-once delivery is a myth and exactly-once processing is not — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0029)

- [ ] Reliability patterns: retries, exponential backoff, jitter, poison messages, dead letter queues, idempotency keys, deduplication windows, ordering guarantees and FIFO queues, backpressure — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0029)

- [ ] SQS vs RabbitMQ vs Kafka: the actual differences. Kafka concepts - partitions, offsets, consumer groups, log compaction, ordering within a partition. When you would choose each — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0029)

- [ ] SYSTEM DESIGN 1: URL shortener. Use all nine steps from Doc 00 Part E. Requirements, scale estimate, API, key generation (counter vs hash vs base62), DB choice, cache, redirect path, analytics — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0029)

- [ ] SYSTEM DESIGN 2: rate limiter. Token bucket, leaky bucket, fixed window, sliding window log, sliding window counter. Distributed rate limiting with Redis. Where it sits in the architecture — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0029)

- [ ] Month 3 cumulative test. SYSTEM DESIGN 3: notification service (email/SMS/push, fan-out, retries, user preferences, deduplication) — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S04-B0029)

- [ ] Weak List repair only. Whatever is weakest gets the whole session — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0014)

- [ ] Full mock loop, self-administered: 2 DSA (25 min each) + 1 system design (45 min) + 3 behavioural, back to back, no breaks — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0014)

- [ ] Weekly test. Review the mock. Identify the 3 biggest gaps and schedule them — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0014)

- [ ] Timed DSA + SYSTEM DESIGN 11: video platform (YouTube) - upload, transcoding pipeline, CDN, recommendations at a high level — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0016)

- [ ] Timed DSA + LLD practice: design a task scheduler in C#, 45 min — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0016)

- [ ] Timed DSA + SYSTEM DESIGN 12: typeahead / autocomplete - tries, ranking, caching, updates — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0016)

- [ ] Timed DSA + behavioural: the hard ones. Failure, conflict, disagreement with a senior, a decision you got wrong — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0016)

- [ ] Weak List repair. Any real interviews this week get analysed in writing — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0016)

- [ ] Pramp mock 1 (DSA) + Pramp mock 2 (system design). Use your free credits deliberately — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0016)

- [ ] Weekly test. Apply to 5-8 strong product companies — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0016)

- [ ] LP intensive 1. Re-read Amazon's own wording for all 16. Map your 20 stories to the matrix. Find LPs with thin coverage — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0019)

- [ ] LP intensive 2. The six high-frequency LPs get two rehearsed stories each. Practise the follow-up probes: "what was the data?", "what would you do differently?", "what did *you* do, not the team?" — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0019)

- [ ] Amazon-style DSA: their tagged patterns lean towards arrays, strings, hashing, trees, graphs, heaps. Timed set of 4 — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0019)

- [ ] Amazon LLD round preparation. They ask you to code a design. Practise 2 timed LLDs with working C# — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0019)

- [ ] Amazon HLD. Design a package tracking system in 20 minutes (their OA format), then a full 45-minute version — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0019)

- [ ] Full Amazon-format mock: DSA + LLD + HLD + LP, in one sitting. This is a long session by design — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0019)

- [ ] Weekly test. Apply to Amazon. Referral first if you have one — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0019)

- [ ] Whiteboard conditions. All DSA this week in a plain text editor: no autocomplete, no compiler, no running the code. Google interviews this way — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0023)

- [ ] Google-style DSA: they lean towards graphs, DP, recursion, and problems with strong follow-up variations. Practise the follow-up: "now make it work on a stream", "now it does not fit in memory" — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0023)

- [ ] Code comprehension preparation. Take an unfamiliar open-source .NET repo. Read a subsystem for 45 minutes and explain it aloud: what it does, how, what you would change, where the bugs might be — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0023)

- [ ] SYSTEM DESIGN 13: Google Docs (collaborative editing, OT/CRDT at a concept level) + 14: distributed cache (consistent hashing, eviction, replication) — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0023)

- [ ] Googliness preparation. Collaboration, ambiguity, disagreement, receiving critical feedback, mentoring, what you do when you are wrong — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0023)

- [ ] Full Google-format mock: 2 coding (no IDE) + 1 system design + 1 behavioural — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0023)

- [ ] Month 6 cumulative test. Apply to Google. Review the whole month honestly — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0023)

- [ ] Am I solving medium problems in under 25 minutes, consistently, narrating throughout? — — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0025)

- [ ] Can I hold a 45-minute system design conversation without freezing or running dry? — — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0025)

- [ ] Have I done at least 4 external mocks (Pramp or peer)? — — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0025)

- [ ] Can I code on a blank text editor without autocomplete without it slowing me down badly? — — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0025)

- [ ] Are all 20 stories rehearsed, randomised, and under 3 minutes each? — — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0025)

- [ ] Do I have a real answer for every high-frequency Amazon LP? — — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0025)

- [ ] Have I applied to Amazon and Google? — — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0025)

- [ ] Is my pipeline still active - 2-3 applications per week going out? — — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0025)

- [ ] Have I analysed every real interview in writing? — — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0025)

- [ ] Questions asked — As close to verbatim as you can remember — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0027)

- [ ] What went well — Be specific, not "it was okay" — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0027)

- [ ] What went badly — The precise moment you stalled — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0027)

- [ ] Named repair action — A concrete topic or drill, scheduled on a date — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0027)

- [ ] Weak List additions — Every gap goes on the list — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S07-B0027)

- [ ] Gap repair block. Take your interview analysis log. Rank gaps by frequency. Attack the top one for the full session — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0013)

- [ ] Kafka in depth: partitions, offsets, consumer groups and rebalancing, ordering guarantees within a partition, log compaction, exactly-once semantics and transactions, replication and ISR — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0013)

- [ ] Timed DSA (2 medium) + SYSTEM DESIGN 15: ad click aggregation - stream processing, windowing, late events, approximate counting — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0013)

- [ ] Stream processing concepts: event time vs processing time, watermarks, tumbling/sliding/session windows, exactly-once in streams, backfill and replay — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0013)

- [ ] Gap repair + Weak List — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0013), [detail 2](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0015), [detail 3](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0019)

- [ ] Full mock loop (2 DSA + 1 design + 1 behavioural, back to back) + RAG project: Phase 1 (Doc 12) — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0013)

- [ ] Weekly test. Interview analysis for the week. Applications out — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0013)

- [ ] Gap repair block — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0015), [detail 2](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0017), [detail 3](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0019)

- [ ] Advanced consistency: linearizability vs serializability (they are not the same), snapshot isolation, write skew, causal consistency, read-your-writes and monotonic reads — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0015)

- [ ] Timed DSA + SYSTEM DESIGN 16: metrics and monitoring system - time series storage, cardinality, downsampling, alerting — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0015)

- [ ] Multi-region and disaster recovery: active-active vs active-passive, RPO/RTO, data residency, geo-routing, cross-region replication lag — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0015)

- [ ] Full mock loop + RAG project: Phase 2 — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0015)

- [ ] Weekly test. Interview analysis. Applications out — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0015), [detail 2](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0017)

- [ ] Amazon-tagged DSA set. Use Sean Prashad's company tags. 4 problems timed — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0017)

- [ ] Google-tagged DSA set, no IDE, plus the follow-up variations Google favours — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0017)

- [ ] SYSTEM DESIGN 17: nearby friends / proximity service - geohashing, quadtrees, location updates at scale — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0017)

- [ ] Gap repair + Weak List. Behavioural: re-record your three weakest stories — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0017)

- [ ] Full mock loop + RAG project: Phase 3 — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S08-B0017)

- [ ] Interview execution — Weekly full loops. Randomised behavioural. Real interviews continuing — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S09-B0007)

- [ ] Oral revision: databases and SQL. Indexes, plans, transactions, isolation, locking, replication, sharding. No notes — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S09-B0009)

- [ ] Weekly test. Applications and follow-ups — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S09-B0009)

- [ ] Weak List repair — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S09-B0011)

- [ ] Full mock loop. Then redo 3 more system designs — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S09-B0011)

- [ ] Redo 3 system designs from scratch, timed, no notes — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S09-B0013)

- [ ] Project defence, second pass. 30 minutes hostile questioning on both projects — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S09-B0013)

- [ ] Weak List. Final gap repair — [detail 1](NOTES/13_ALL_STUDY_LESSONS.md#S09-B0013)

## Both full project specifications and resource directory


- [ ] Microsoft Learn - .NET learn.microsoft.com/dotnet — The canonical reference. Language guide, runtime, ASP.NET Core, EF Core. Start every topic here — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0008)

- [ ] .NET Blog devblogs.microsoft.com/dotnet — Deep engineering posts from the team that builds it — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0008)

- [ ] How Async/Await Really Works (Stephen Toub, .NET Blog) — The single best article on async. Read it three times across the plan — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0008)

- [ ] Deep .NET (YouTube, Microsoft Developer) — Toub and Hanselman on async, spans, GC, LINQ internals. Best deep-dive video series that exists — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0008)

- [ ] SharpLab sharplab.io — Paste C#, see the compiler-generated code. Use it to actually see the async state machine and closures — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0008)

- [ ] source.dot.net — Browse the real .NET source. When docs are ambiguous, read the implementation — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0008)

- [ ] Book of the Runtime (BotR) in github.com/dotnet/runtime docs — CLR internals: type loader, GC design, JIT. Month 2 material — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0008)

- [ ] Stephen Cleary's blog blog.stephencleary.com — The authority on async pitfalls, deadlocks, ConfigureAwait — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0008)

- [ ] Konrad Kokosa tooslowexception.com — .NET memory and GC internals — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0008)

- [ ] Nick Chapsas (YouTube) — Modern C#, performance, testing, clean patterns. Free videos — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0008)

- [ ] Milan Jovanovic (YouTube + blog) — .NET architecture, DDD, clean architecture, CQRS — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0008)

- [ ] Tim Corey (YouTube) — Long-form fundamentals, well paced for filling gaps — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0008)

- [ ] Exercism C# track exercism.org — ~150 free exercises with human mentoring. Excellent for language fluency — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0008)

- [ ] BenchmarkDotNet benchmarkdotnet.org — Prove your optimisation claims with numbers. Needed for the project — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0008)

- [ ] NeetCode neetcode.io — Free roadmap + NeetCode 150 + video for every problem. Your primary DSA spine — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0010)

- [ ] NeetCode YouTube — Explanations that build intuition rather than reciting code — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0010)

- [ ] Grind 75 techinterviewhandbook.org/grind75 — Configurable ordered list by weeks available. Set it to your timeline — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0010)

- [ ] Blind 75 — The classic minimum set. ~85% overlap with NeetCode 150 — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0010)

- [ ] Tech Interview Handbook techinterviewhandbook.org — Free and genuinely excellent: algorithms, behavioural, resume, negotiation — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0010)

- [ ] Sean Prashad's Leetcode Patterns seanprashad.com/leetcode-patterns — Problems grouped by pattern with company tags. Free — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0010)

- [ ] LeetCode free tier leetcode.com — Where you actually solve. Free tier is sufficient - premium is not required — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0010)

- [ ] CSES Problem Set cses.fi/problemset — Free, high quality, strong on graphs and DP — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0010)

- [ ] VisuAlgo visualgo.net — Watch the data structure move. Use when a structure will not click — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0010)

- [ ] Big-O Cheat Sheet bigocheatsheet.com — Complexity reference for common operations — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0010)

- [ ] MIT OCW 6.006 — University-grade algorithms if you want theory beneath the patterns — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0010)

- [ ] ByteByteGo (YouTube) — Short, clear, visual explanations of every core concept. Free videos — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0012)

- [ ] Hello Interview hellointerview.com — Free written system design guides and a strong delivery framework — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0012)

- [ ] Martin Kleppmann's distributed systems lectures (YouTube, Cambridge) — Free lecture series + free PDF notes. The rigorous option — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0012)

- [ ] MIT 6.5840 Distributed Systems — Raft, consistency, fault tolerance. Free lectures and labs — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0012)

- [ ] High Performance Browser Networking hpbn.co — Free full book by Ilya Grigorik. TCP, TLS, HTTP/2, WebSockets. Excellent — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0014)

- [ ] Beej's Guide to Network Programming beej.us/guide/bgnet — Sockets from the ground up, free — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0014)

- [ ] MDN HTTP docs developer.mozilla.org — Methods, status codes, headers, caching, CORS — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0014)

- [ ] Use The Index, Luke use-the-index-luke.com — Free book on SQL indexing and execution plans. Directly upgrades your day job — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0014)

- [ ] PostgreSQL documentation — MVCC, isolation levels, EXPLAIN. Among the best DB docs written — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0014)

- [ ] SQLBolt sqlbolt.com / PG Exercises pgexercises.com — Interactive SQL practice, free — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0014)

- [ ] OSTEP pages.cs.wisc.edu/~remzi/OSTEP — Free operating systems textbook. Processes, threads, scheduling, concurrency — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0014)

- [ ] Refactoring Guru refactoring.guru — Design patterns and refactoring with C# examples. Clear and free — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0016)

- [ ] SourceMaking sourcemaking.com — Patterns and anti-patterns — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0016)

- [ ] Microsoft .NET architecture guides learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/architecture — Free e-books: microservices, DDD, containerised apps — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0016)

- [ ] Awesome Low Level Design (GitHub) — LLD problem statements: parking lot, elevator, rate limiter, splitwise — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0016)

- [ ] GitHub Actions docs docs.github.com/actions — Your CI/CD spine for the project. Free minutes on public repos — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0018)

- [ ] Play with Docker labs.play-with-docker.com — Free browser Docker sandbox, no local install needed — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0018)

- [ ] AWS Free Tier aws.amazon.com/free — Where the project gets deployed. Set a billing alarm on day one — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0018)

- [ ] AWS Skill Builder (free tier) — Free official courses on SQS, SES, EventBridge, ECS, RDS — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0018)

- [ ] k6 k6.io — Free open source load testing. Required for the project's performance report — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0018)

- [ ] Prometheus + Grafana docs — Metrics and dashboards for the project's observability phase — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0018)

- [ ] Serilog serilog.net — Structured logging in .NET — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0018)
