- [ ] Pramp pramp.com — Peer-to-peer mocks, matched with another engineer, you interview each other. Free tier is now capped around 5 credits per month - budget them — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0021)

- [ ] Google Interview Warmup grow.google/certificates/interview-warmup — Fully free, no paywall. Good for behavioural and speaking fluency — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0021)

- [ ] interviewing.io — Paid mocks ($179+, more for FAANG engineers) - skip the paid tier. But their free recorded real interviews are genuinely valuable: watch how strong candidates narrate — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0021)

- [ ] Discord / Reddit study groups (r/leetcode, cscareerquestions) — Free peer mock partners. Quality varies; a consistent partner is worth a lot — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0021)

- [ ] A friend or colleague — Underrated. Even a non-specialist can time you and detect rambling — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0021)

- [ ] Self-recording — Phone camera, 45 minutes, one problem, narrate throughout. Watch it back once — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0021)

- [ ] Month — Mocks — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0023)

- [ ] M1-M4 — Self-recording only. Weekly. You are building content, not yet performing — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0023)

- [ ] M5 — 2 Pramp sessions. Expect them to go badly. That is data, not failure — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0023)

- [ ] M6 — 4-6 mocks: 2 DSA, 2 system design, 2 behavioural. Plus real interviews at smaller companies as calibration — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0023)

- [ ] M7 — Weekly full loop simulation: DSA + design + behavioural in one sitting — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0023)

- [ ] M8 — Targeted mocks only on your remaining weak areas — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0023)

- [ ] Amazon Leadership Principles amazon.jobs (our-workplace/leadership-principles) — Read Amazon's own wording. Do not learn them second-hand — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0025)

- [ ] Amazon interviewing resources on amazon.jobs — Amazon publishes its own STAR guidance. Free and authoritative — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0025)

- [ ] Tech Interview Handbook - behavioural — Free, structured question bank — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0025)

- [ ] Levels.fyi — Level calibration and compensation reality before you negotiate — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0025)

- [ ] Glassdoor / LeetCode Discuss — Recent real interview reports. Filter by date - anything older than a year is stale — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0025)

- [ ] Tool — Use — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0027)

- [ ] Anki apps.ankiweb.net — Spaced repetition. Free on desktop and Android. Max 15 min/day — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0027)

- [ ] Obsidian or plain Word/Markdown — Master Notes. Do not over-engineer this; the notes matter, not the app — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0027)

- [ ] Excalidraw — System design diagrams, and diagrams for your project README — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0027)

- [ ] Phone voice recorder — The Explain-Aloud protocol. Already on your phone — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0027)

- [ ] A plain text editor (Notepad, VS Code with everything disabled) — Whiteboard-condition DSA practice from Month 3 — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0027)

- [ ] Git + GitHub — The project, and a green contribution graph as passive evidence — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0027)

- [ ] Skip — Why — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0030)

- [ ] Paid courses (Educative, Interview Kickstart, AlgoExpert, Scaler) — Everything above covers the same ground free. Documented cases exist of free-only preparation reaching the same outcomes — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0030)

- [ ] LeetCode Premium — Useful but not necessary. Sean Prashad's list gives you company tags free — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0030)

- [ ] Learning a second language (Go, Rust, Python) — You interview in C#. Both companies accept it. Splitting focus is pure cost — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0030)

- [ ] Competitive programming (Codeforces contests) — Different skill. Interview DSA is easier and narrower — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0030)

- [ ] General spoken-English courses — Your gap is technical vocabulary under pressure, not grammar — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0030)

- [ ] Certifications (AZ-204, AWS SAA) — Neither Google nor Amazon weighs them meaningfully for SWE — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0030)

- [ ] Rebuilding your resume every month — Once in Month 1, polish in Month 6. It is not preparation — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0030)

- [ ] New framework tourism — Depth in .NET beats breadth across five stacks — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S01-B0030)

- [ ] Capability — Must demonstrate — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0010)

- [ ] Durable submission — Job survives an API restart between submission and processing — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0010)

- [ ] Asynchronous processing — Real queue, real separate worker process. Not an in-memory BackgroundService pretending — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0010)

- [ ] Horizontal scaling — Run 1 worker, then 4. Throughput must measurably increase. With numbers — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0010)

- [ ] Crash recovery — Kill a worker mid-job. The job must be recovered and completed, not lost — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0010)

- [ ] Idempotency — Deliver the same message twice. The job must execute once — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0010)

- [ ] Retries — Exponential backoff with jitter, bounded attempts — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0010)

- [ ] Dead letter queue — Poison messages land in a DLQ, are inspectable, and can be replayed — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0010)

- [ ] Priority — High-priority jobs preempt a backlog of low-priority jobs — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0010)

- [ ] Fairness — One tenant flooding the queue must not starve others — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0010)

- [ ] Rate limiting — Correct across multiple API instances, not per-instance — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0010)

- [ ] Multi-tenancy — Tenant A provably cannot read or affect tenant B's jobs — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0010)

- [ ] Observability — Structured logs with correlation IDs, metrics, health checks — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0010)

- [ ] Deployment — Docker, CI/CD, running on AWS — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0010)

- [ ] Evidence — Load test results with p50/p95/p99, and a written bottleneck analysis — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0010)

- [ ] Component — Responsibility — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0013)

- [ ] API service (ASP.NET Core) — Job submission, status queries, DLQ inspection and replay, auth, rate limiting — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0013)

- [ ] Worker service (.NET worker) — Consumes from the queue, executes handlers, manages state transitions, heartbeats — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0013)

- [ ] PostgreSQL — Durable job metadata, state, attempt history, idempotency keys, outbox — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0013)

- [ ] Queue (SQS in AWS; RabbitMQ locally) — Work distribution, visibility timeout, DLQ — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0013)

- [ ] Redis — Status read cache, distributed rate limiting, worker registry — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0013)

- [ ] Prometheus + Grafana — Metrics and dashboards — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0013)

- [ ] State — Transitions to — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0016)

- [ ] Pending — Queued (on successful enqueue), Cancelled — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0016)

- [ ] Queued — Running (worker claims it), Cancelled — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0016)

- [ ] Running — Completed, Failed, Retrying, Orphaned (heartbeat lost) — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0016)

- [ ] Retrying — Queued (after backoff), DeadLettered (attempts exhausted) — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0016)

- [ ] Orphaned — Queued (reclaimed by the reaper) — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0016)

- [ ] Failed — DeadLettered — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0016)

- [ ] Completed / Cancelled / DeadLettered — Terminal — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0016)

- [ ] Problem — Your solution — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0018)

- [ ] Duplicate delivery. The queue guarantees at-least-once, so the same message will eventually arrive twice — Idempotency key per job. A processed_jobs table with a unique constraint. Worker attempts an insert first; a unique-violation means already processed, so acknowledge and skip — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0018)

- [ ] Worker crash mid-job. The job is Running but nobody is running it — Worker heartbeats every N seconds. A reaper process finds Running jobs whose heartbeat is stale past a threshold, transitions them to Orphaned, then re-queues. Combined with idempotency, a job that actually completed before the crash will not re-run its side effects — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0018)

- [ ] Dual write. You must both save the job and enqueue the message, and either can fail — Transactional outbox: write job and outbox row in one DB transaction, then a relay publishes from the outbox and marks it sent. At-least-once publish, made safe by idempotency — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0018)

- [ ] Section — Content — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0055)

- [ ] One-paragraph pitch — The memorised sentence from page 1 — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0055)

- [ ] Architecture diagram — Excalidraw, embedded as an image — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0055)

- [ ] Why this exists — The problem it solves. Not "a demo project" — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0055)

- [ ] Design decisions — Each with the alternative you rejected and why — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0055)

- [ ] Failure modes table — Failure, detection, response, observed behaviour — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0055)

- [ ] Performance results — Load test numbers, p50/p95/p99, throughput at 1 vs 4 workers — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0055)

- [ ] Bottleneck analysis — Where it breaks, why, evidenced — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0055)

- [ ] Trade-offs and limitations — Be honest. Stating a known limitation is a senior signal — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0055)

- [ ] What I would do next — Shows you know where the edges are — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0055)

- [ ] How to run it — docker compose up and it works. Test this on a clean machine — [detail 1](NOTES/14_PROJECTS_AND_RESOURCES.md#S10-B0055)

## Every original curriculum branch: basics through advanced


- [ ] CodeLLDWithShubham Mindmap:** OOP Fundamentals, 5 Class Relationships, 8 Design Principles, 5 SOLID Principles, 5 UML Diagrams, and all 23 Gang of Four (GoF) Design Patterns. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0002)

- [ ] DSA Patterns (CodeWithShubham) Mindmap:** Arrays, Strings (KMP, Rabin-Karp), Stacks, Queues, Linked Lists, Recursion & Backtracking, Trees (Views, Traversals, BST), Heaps, Graphs (Shortest Paths, Topo Sort, DSU, Bipartite), Trie, DP (1D, Knapsack, LCS, LIS, Grid, MCM), Greedy, Bit Manipulation, all 8 Sorting Algorithms, and Range Queries (Segment & Fenwick Trees). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0002)

- [ ] Classes & Objects:** Blueprints, memory instantiation on heap vs stack in C#, object lifecycle. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0005)

- [ ] Constructors:** Default, parameterized, copy constructors, static constructors in C#, constructor chaining (`this()`, `base()`). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0005)

- [ ] Encapsulation:** Data hiding, information hiding, getters/setters, properties with backing fields. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0005)

- [ ] Abstraction:** Hiding implementation complexity, exposing essential contracts via interfaces and abstract base classes. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0005)

- [ ] Inheritance:** `is-a` relationship, single vs multiple interface inheritance in C#, `virtual`, `override`, `new` keywords. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0005)

- [ ] Polymorphism:** — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0005)

- [ ] Compile-time (Static): Method overloading, operator overloading. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0005)

- [ ] Runtime (Dynamic): Method overriding via virtual method table (vtable). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0005)

- [ ] Abstract Classes:** Partial implementation, abstract methods, when to use vs interfaces. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0005)

- [ ] Access Modifiers:** `public`, `private`, `protected`, `internal`, `protected internal`, `private protected`. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0005)

- [ ] Static:** Static classes, static methods, static constructors, static fields (thread safety considerations). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0005)

- [ ] Enums:** Strongly typed constants, flags enums (`[Flags]`), underlying types (`byte`, `int`). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0005)

- [ ] Association (`-->`):** General `uses-a` relationship between two independent classes (e.g., `Order` uses `EmailService`). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0007)

- [ ] Dependency (`..>`):** Weakest relationship; one class depends on another as a method parameter or local variable (temporary interaction). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0007)

- [ ] Aggregation (`◇--`):** `has-a` relationship with **weak ownership** (Whole-Part). The child can exist independently of the parent (e.g., `Department` has `Professors`; if department dissolves, professors still exist). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0007)

- [ ] Composition (`◆--`):** `has-a` relationship with **strong ownership**. The child's lifetime is bound to the parent's lifetime (e.g., `House` and `Rooms`; destroying the house destroys the rooms). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0007)

- [ ] Realization (`..▷`):** Interface implementation or abstract contract realization (`class Car : IVehicle`). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0007)

- [ ] DRY (Don't Repeat Yourself):** Extract reusable logic; single source of truth for business rules. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0009)

- [ ] KISS (Keep It Simple, Stupid):** Avoid premature optimization; prefer simple, readable code over clever one-liners. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0009)

- [ ] YAGNI (You Aren't Gonna Need It):** Implement only what is currently required; avoid building speculative features. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0009)

- [ ] Separation of Concerns (SoC):** Break software into distinct sections where each section addresses a separate concern (e.g., Presentation vs Business Logic vs Data Access). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0009)

- [ ] Coupling (Loose vs Tight):** Measure of dependency between modules. Aim for loose coupling via interfaces. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0009)

- [ ] Cohesion (High vs Low):** Measure of how strongly related the responsibilities inside a single class are. Aim for high cohesion (a class does one cohesive job). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0009)

- [ ] Composition Over Inheritance:** Prefer composing objects with interfaces rather than inheriting deep hierarchies (avoids fragile base class problem). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0009)

- [ ] Law of Demeter (Principle of Least Knowledge):** A method of an object should only call methods belonging to: its own class, parameters passed in, objects it instantiated, or its direct member fields. Don't chain calls like `a.GetB().GetC().DoSomething()`. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0009)

- [ ] 1. **SRP (Single Responsibility Principle):** A class should have only one reason to change. Separate domain entities from persistence, formatting, and validation. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0011)

- [ ] 2. **OCP (Open-Closed Principle):** Software entities should be open for extension, but closed for modification. Implement new behavior by adding new classes/strategies, not modifying existing tested code. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0011)

- [ ] 3. **LSP (Liskov Substitution Principle):** Subtypes must be substitutable for their base types without altering program correctness (e.g., classic Rectangle vs Square violation). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0011)

- [ ] 4. **ISP (Interface Segregation Principle):** Clients should not be forced to depend on interfaces they do not use. Split fat interfaces into smaller, cohesive role interfaces (`IReadable`, `IWritable` rather than `IReadWriteAdminSuperService`). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0011)

- [ ] 5. **DIP (Dependency Inversion Principle):** High-level modules should not depend on low-level modules; both should depend on abstractions. Abstractions should not depend on details; details should depend on abstractions (Dependency Injection). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0011)

- [ ] Class Diagram:** Visualizing classes, fields, methods, access modifiers (`+`, `-`, `#`), and relationships (Inheritance, Composition, Aggregation, Dependency). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0013)

- [ ] Use Case Diagram:** Actors, system boundaries, use cases, `<<include>>` and `<<extend>>` relationships. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0013)

- [ ] Sequence Diagram:** Chronological interaction between objects over time; lifelines, synchronous messages, asynchronous calls, return messages, activation bars. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0013)

- [ ] Activity Diagram:** Workflow and business logic flowchart; decision diamonds, fork/join parallel nodes. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0013)

- [ ] State Machine Diagram:** States, transitions, events, guards, actions (critical for designing Elevators, Vending Machines, Order fulfillment). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0013)

- [ ] 1. **Singleton:** Ensures a class has only one instance, with a global access point. In C#: Thread-safe double-checked locking or `Lazy<T>`. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0016)

- [ ] 2. **Builder:** Separates the construction of a complex object from its representation (e.g., fluent query builder, complex document generator). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0016)

- [ ] 3. **Factory Method:** Defines an interface for creating an object, but lets subclasses decide which class to instantiate. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0016)

- [ ] 4. **Abstract Factory:** Creates families of related or dependent objects without specifying their concrete classes (e.g., DarkTheme vs LightTheme UI components). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0016)

- [ ] 5. **Prototype:** Creates new objects by cloning an existing instance (in C#: `ICloneable` or memberwise clone). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0016)

- [ ] 1. **Strategy:** Defines a family of algorithms, encapsulates each one, and makes them interchangeable at runtime (e.g., Payment strategies, Pricing rules). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0017)

- [ ] 2. **Iterator:** Provides a way to access elements of an aggregate object sequentially without exposing its underlying representation (`IEnumerator<T>` in C#). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0017)

- [ ] 3. **Command:** Encapsulates a request as an object, thereby letting you parameterize clients with different requests, queue or log requests, and support undo/redo. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0017)

- [ ] 4. **Observer:** Defines a one-to-many dependency so that when one object changes state, all its dependents are notified automatically (Event-driven architectures). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0017)

- [ ] 5. **State:** Allows an object to alter its behavior when its internal state changes (e.g., Elevator: `IdleState`, `MovingUpState`, `MovingDownState`). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0017)

- [ ] 6. **Template Method:** Defines the skeleton of an algorithm in a method, deferring some steps to subclasses without changing algorithm structure. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0017)

- [ ] 7. **Chain of Responsibility:** Passes a request along a chain of handlers. Each handler decides either to process the request or pass it to the next handler (e.g., ASP.NET Core Middleware pipeline). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0017)

- [ ] 8. **Visitor:** Separates an algorithm from the object structure on which it operates (e.g., Tax calculation across different product types). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0017)

- [ ] 9. **Mediator:** Defines an object that encapsulates how a set of objects interact, preventing direct dependencies between them (e.g., MediatR library in .NET, Air Traffic Controller). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0017)

- [ ] 10. **Memento:** Captures and externalizes an object's internal state so the object can be restored to this state later (Undo/Redo checkpoints). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0017)

- [ ] 1. **Adapter:** Converts the interface of a class into another interface clients expect (wrapping legacy APIs or third-party SDKs). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0018)

- [ ] 2. **Facade:** Provides a simplified, unified high-level interface to a complex subsystem. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0018)

- [ ] 3. **Decorator:** Attaches additional responsibilities to an object dynamically without subclass explosion (e.g., CachingDecorator, LoggingDecorator around repositories). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0018)

- [ ] 4. **Composite:** Composes objects into tree structures to represent part-whole hierarchies. Lets clients treat individual objects and compositions uniformly (e.g., File System folders and files). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0018)

- [ ] 5. **Proxy:** Provides a surrogate or placeholder for another object to control access to it (e.g., Virtual Proxy for lazy loading, Remote Proxy, Protection Proxy). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0018)

- [ ] 6. **Bridge:** Decouples an abstraction from its implementation so that the two can vary independently (e.g., `Shape` and `Color` hierarchies). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0018)

- [ ] 7. **Flyweight:** Reduces memory footprint by sharing common state among multiple fine-grained objects (e.g., Character glyph rendering in text editors, game particle systems). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0018)

- [ ] Kadane’s Algorithm:** Max Subarray Sum, Max Product Subarray (tracking `min` & `max`), Circular Subarray Sum (Max of normal subarray vs `TotalSum - MinSubarray`). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0021)

- [ ] Prefix Sum:** 1D Prefix Sum, 2D Prefix Sum (Matrix range queries), Subarray Sum Equals K (`Map<sum - k, count>`). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0021)

- [ ] Two Pointers:** Opposite Ends, Fast and Slow (Reader-Writer), 3Sum (duplicate skipping logic), 4Sum, Container With Most Water, Trapping Rain Water. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0021)

- [ ] Dutch National Flag:** 3-way partition (`low`, `mid`, `high`) sorting 0, 1, 2 in $O(N)$ single pass. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0021)

- [ ] Sliding Window:** Fixed size, Variable size, At Most $K$ difference technique. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0021)

- [ ] Intervals & Matrix:** Merge Intervals, Insert Interval, Non-Overlapping Intervals, Spiral Matrix, Rotate Image. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0021)

- [ ] Two Pointers / Palindromes:** Valid Palindrome, Longest Palindromic Substring (expand around center vs DP). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0023)

- [ ] Sliding Window on Strings:** Longest Substring Without Repeating Characters, Minimum Window Substring, Find All Anagrams in a String. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0023)

- [ ] Pattern Matching Algorithms:** — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0023)

- [ ] KMP (Knuth-Morris-Pratt):** Precomputing the Longest Prefix Suffix (LPS) array in $O(M)$ time to search patterns in $O(N)$ time without backtracking. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0023)

- [ ] Rabin-Karp:** Rolling hash matching. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0023)

- [ ] Z-Algorithm:** Constructing the Z-array in $O(N)$ linear time. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0023)

- [ ] String Hashing & Frequency:** Group Anagrams (Prime hashing vs sorted key). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0023)

- [ ] Monotonic Stack:** Next Greater Element I & II, Previous Smaller Element, Largest Rectangle in Histogram ($O(N)$ height expansion), Daily Temperatures. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0025)

- [ ] Balanced Parentheses:** Valid Parentheses, Minimum Remove to Make Valid Parentheses, Longest Valid Parentheses. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0025)

- [ ] Min Stack:** $O(1)$ `GetMin()` using auxiliary min-stack or value encoding (`2 * val - min`). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0025)

- [ ] Expression Evaluation:** Infix to Postfix conversion, Evaluating Reverse Polish Notation (RPN), Basic Calculator I & II. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0025)

- [ ] Circular Queue:** Ring buffer array implementation with `head` and `tail` pointers. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0027)

- [ ] Monotonic Deque:** Sliding Window Maximum ($O(N)$ double-ended queue). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0027)

- [ ] Queue using Stacks & Stack using Queues:** Amortized $O(1)$ push/pop mechanisms. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0027)

- [ ] Reversals:** Iterative reversal, Recursive reversal, Reverse Linked List II (between $m$ and $n$), Reverse Nodes in k-Group. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0029)

- [ ] Fast & Slow Pointers:** Middle of Linked List, Detect Cycle, Find Cycle Origin (Floyd’s Tortoise and Hare proof). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0029)

- [ ] Merging:** Merge Two Sorted Lists, Merge K Sorted Lists (Min-Heap approach in $O(N \log K)$). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0029)

- [ ] Pointer Mutation:** Remove Nth Node From End of List, Reorder List, Palindrome Linked List. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0029)

- [ ] Doubly Linked List:** Implementing an LRU Cache & LFU Cache in $O(1)$ time. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0029)

- [ ] Subsets & Subsequences:** Subsets I (distinct elements), Subsets II (handling duplicates with sorting and skip loop). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0031)

- [ ] Combinations:** Combination Sum I, II, III. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0031)

- [ ] Permutations:** Permutations I, Permutations II (swap in-place vs visited array). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0031)

- [ ] Board & Matrix Backtracking:** Word Search, Rat in a Maze, N-Queens (validating diagonals and columns), Sudoku Solver. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0031)

- [ ] Traversals:** Inorder, Preorder, Postorder (Recursive + Iterative using Stack), Level Order (BFS), Zigzag Level Order, Boundary Traversal. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0033)

- [ ] Tree Views:** Top View (Horizontal distance tracking), Bottom View, Left View, Right View. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0033)

- [ ] Tree DP (Bottom-Up):** Maximum Depth, Diameter of Binary Tree, Balanced Binary Tree, Binary Tree Maximum Path Sum, Lowest Common Ancestor (LCA). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0033)

- [ ] Construction & Serialization:** Construct from Inorder + Preorder, Construct from Inorder + Postorder, Serialize and Deserialize Binary Tree. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0033)

- [ ] Binary Search Tree (BST):** Validate BST (passing down valid range bounds), Kth Smallest Element, Inorder Successor, LCA of BST, Convert Sorted Array to BST. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0033)

- [ ] Min-Heap & Max-Heap Basics:** Array representation (`parent = (i-1)/2`, `left = 2i+1`, `right = 2i+2`), Heapify operations. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0035)

- [ ] Top-K Elements:** Kth Largest Element in an Array, Top K Frequent Elements. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0035)

- [ ] Two Heaps Pattern:** Find Median from Data Stream (balancing sizes of Max-Heap and Min-Heap). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0035)

- [ ] K-Way Merge:** Merge K Sorted Lists, Smallest Range Covering Elements from K Lists. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0035)

- [ ] Representations:** Adjacency Matrix vs Adjacency List. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0037)

- [ ] BFS & DFS:** Number of Islands, Max Area of Island, Rotting Oranges (Multi-source BFS), Clone Graph. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0037)

- [ ] Cycle Detection:** — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0037)

- [ ] Undirected: BFS/DFS with parent pointer, or Disjoint Set Union (DSU). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0037)

- [ ] Directed: 3-Coloring DFS (0 = Unvisited, 1 = Visiting in current path, 2 = Completely visited). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0037)

- [ ] Topological Sort (DAG):** Kahn’s Algorithm (In-degree queue), Course Schedule I & II, Alien Dictionary. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0037)

- [ ] Shortest Path Algorithms:** — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0037)

- [ ] Unweighted: Standard BFS ($O(V + E)$). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0037)

- [ ] Non-Negative Weighted: Dijkstra’s with Min-Heap ($O((V + E) \log V)$). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0037)

- [ ] Negative Weights: Bellman-Ford (detecting negative cycles in $O(V \cdot E)$). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0037)

- [ ] All-Pairs Shortest Path: Floyd-Warshall ($O(V^3)$). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0037)

- [ ] Disjoint Set Union (DSU / Union-Find):** Path Compression + Union by Rank ($O(\alpha(N))$), Number of Connected Components, Redundant Connection, Kruskal’s Minimum Spanning Tree (MST). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0037)

- [ ] Bipartite Graph:** 2-Coloring via BFS/DFS (Is Graph Bipartite?). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0037)

- [ ] Strongly Connected Components (SCC):** Kosaraju’s Algorithm (2-pass DFS with transposed graph) & Tarjan’s Algorithm. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0037)

- [ ] Prefix Tree:** Insert, Search, StartsWith in $O(L)$ time. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0039)

- [ ] Applications:** Word Search II (Trie + Backtracking grid search), Search Suggestions System. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0039)

- [ ] Bitwise Trie:** Maximum XOR of Two Numbers in an Array ($O(32N)$ time). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0039)

- [ ] 1D Linear State:** Fibonacci, Climbing Stairs, House Robber I & II, Decode Ways. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0041)

- [ ] 0/1 Knapsack:** Subset Sum, Partition Equal Subset Sum, Target Sum (traversing capacity backwards). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0041)

- [ ] Unbounded Knapsack:** Coin Change I & II, Rod Cutting (traversing capacity forwards). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0041)

- [ ] Longest Common Subsequence (LCS):** LCS, Edit Distance, Longest Palindromic Subsequence, Shortest Common Supersequence. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0041)

- [ ] Longest Increasing Subsequence (LIS):** $O(N^2)$ DP table and $O(N \log N)$ Binary Search using Patience Sorting / Tails array. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0041)

- [ ] Grid DP:** Unique Paths I & II, Minimum Path Sum, Cherry Pickup. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0041)

- [ ] Interval DP & MCM:** Matrix Chain Multiplication, Burst Balloons, Palindrome Partitioning II. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0041)

- [ ] DP on Trees & Bitmask DP:** Tree independent sets, Traveling Salesperson Problem (TSP) on small $N \le 16$. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0041)

- [ ] Interval Scheduling:** Activity Selection, Non-overlapping Intervals, Minimum Number of Arrows to Burst Balloons. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0043)

- [ ] Array Greedy:** Jump Game I (maximum reachable index) & Jump Game II, Gas Station (circular greedy balance). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0043)

- [ ] XOR Invariants:** $a \oplus a = 0$, $a \oplus 0 = a$. Single Number I, II (bit counters modulo 3), and III (isolating lowest set bit `diff & -diff`). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0045)

- [ ] Bitwise Tricks:** Checking if $K$-th bit is set, Brian Kernighan’s Algorithm for counting set bits (`n = n & (n - 1)`), Checking Power of 2 (`n > 0 && (n & (n - 1)) == 0`), Reverse Bits. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0045)

- [ ] Comparison Sorts:** — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0047)

- [ ] Bubble Sort:** $O(N^2)$ adjacent swaps with early-exit flag. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0047)

- [ ] Selection Sort:** $O(N^2)$ finding minimum element in unsorted partition. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0047)

- [ ] Insertion Sort:** $O(N^2)$ shifting elements; near $O(N)$ for almost-sorted arrays. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0047)

- [ ] Merge Sort:** $O(N \log N)$ divide-and-conquer; stable; requires $O(N)$ auxiliary memory. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0047)

- [ ] Quick Sort:** $O(N \log N)$ average, $O(N^2)$ worst case; in-place; partitioning schemes (Lomuto vs Hoare). — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0047)

- [ ] Heap Sort:** $O(N \log N)$ in-place using Max-Heap; unstable. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0047)

- [ ] Non-Comparison Sorts:** — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0047)

- [ ] Counting Sort:** $O(N + K)$ non-comparison sorting for bounded integer keys. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0047)

- [ ] Radix Sort:** $O(D \cdot (N + K))$ digit-by-digit sorting using Counting Sort as a stable subroutine. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0047)

- [ ] Segment Tree:** — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0049)

- [ ] Complete binary tree over an array supporting Range Sum / Range Minimum Query and Point Updates in $O(\log N)$ time. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0049)

- [ ] Lazy Propagation for Range Updates in $O(\log N)$. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0049)

- [ ] Fenwick Tree (Binary Indexed Tree - BIT):** — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0049)

- [ ] Space-efficient array-based tree using two's complement lowest set bit (`i & -i`) for Prefix Sum queries and Point Updates in $O(\log N)$ time with minimal code. — [detail 1](NOTES/15_FULL_CURRICULUM_ENCYCLOPEDIA.md#F02-B0049)

## Original tracker cells, schedules and practice templates


- [ ] Artefact — Where it lives — [detail 1](NOTES/16_TRACKERS_AND_TEMPLATES.md#S11-B0005)

- [ ] Master Notes — One document, one page per concept, six-question frame — [detail 1](NOTES/16_TRACKERS_AND_TEMPLATES.md#S11-B0005)

- [ ] Weak List — Spreadsheet tab. The most important tab — [detail 1](NOTES/16_TRACKERS_AND_TEMPLATES.md#S11-B0005)

- [ ] DSA Log — Spreadsheet tab, one row per problem — [detail 1](NOTES/16_TRACKERS_AND_TEMPLATES.md#S11-B0005)

- [ ] Spaced Review Tracker — Spreadsheet tab, driven by dates — [detail 1](NOTES/16_TRACKERS_AND_TEMPLATES.md#S11-B0005)

- [ ] Work Journal — Plain text file, one line per working day, append only — [detail 1](NOTES/16_TRACKERS_AND_TEMPLATES.md#S11-B0005)

- [ ] STAR Story Bank — One document, one page per story — [detail 1](NOTES/16_TRACKERS_AND_TEMPLATES.md#S11-B0005)

- [ ] Interview Analysis Log — Spreadsheet tab, from Month 6 — [detail 1](NOTES/16_TRACKERS_AND_TEMPLATES.md#S11-B0005)

- [ ] Application Tracker — Spreadsheet tab, from Month 6 — [detail 1](NOTES/16_TRACKERS_AND_TEMPLATES.md#S11-B0005)

- [ ] Field — Fill in — [detail 1](NOTES/16_TRACKERS_AND_TEMPLATES.md#S11-B0007)

- [ ] Date learned — — [detail 1](NOTES/16_TRACKERS_AND_TEMPLATES.md#S11-B0007)

- [ ] What is it? — One sentence, junior-developer level — [detail 1](NOTES/16_TRACKERS_AND_TEMPLATES.md#S11-B0007)

- [ ] Why does it exist? — What problem forced its invention — [detail 1](NOTES/16_TRACKERS_AND_TEMPLATES.md#S11-B0007)

- [ ] Mental model — The analogy or picture you use — [detail 1](NOTES/16_TRACKERS_AND_TEMPLATES.md#S11-B0007)

- [ ] How does it work internally? — The mechanism. This is the interview level — [detail 1](NOTES/16_TRACKERS_AND_TEMPLATES.md#S11-B0007)

- [ ] Example — Smallest code or scenario that demonstrates it — [detail 1](NOTES/16_TRACKERS_AND_TEMPLATES.md#S11-B0007)

- [ ] Real use from my work — Casepoint, Vision, Zibma, or a project. Always fill this in — [detail 1](NOTES/16_TRACKERS_AND_TEMPLATES.md#S11-B0007)
